Fields: `volume_text`, `volume`, `gain_text`, `gain`


## `lcgf.widgets_range` (src/widgets/range.zig)

Range slider: two values between two ends, low and high, each set by its own thumb.

### Top level

- `Bind = [2]f32`: The type of a range slider's bound field: low, high.
- `thumb_rows = 3`: Spec (and tree) rows each thumb takes: the thumb's half of the track and its two pieces.
- `fn bindError(comptime State: type, comptime name: []const u8) ?[]const u8`: Why `State`'s field `name` cannot be a range slider's values, or null when it can.
- `fn thumbId(track: Id, index: u32) Id`: The id of thumb `index` (0 low, 1 high) of the range slider named `track`.
- `fn move(value: [2]f32, i: u1, to: f32) [2]f32`: `value` with thumb `i` moved to `to`, kept on its side of the other thumb: the low thumb stops at the high one and the high at the low (APG multi-thumb slider: each thumb's range ends at the other).
- `fn step(value: [2]f32, i: u1, held: bool, along: f32, focused: bool, lo: f32, hi: f32, in: *const Input) [2]f32`: The values after this frame's input on thumb `i`: while held it follows the pointer at `along` (0 at the track's left end, 1 at its right, clamped); while focused the slider's keys move it (`slider.keyed`, steps of the whole range); either way it stays on its side.
- `fn trackStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The track: as a slider's (`slider.sliderTrack`) but no role of its own; its thumbs carry the semantics.
- `fn parts(th: *const Theme, f0: f32, f1: f32) [2 * thumb_rows]Style`: The styles of the rows under the track, in row order, for thumbs at fractions `f0 <= f1` of the track: low half (to the midpoint between the thumbs; role slider, as each thumb is), its outside piece, its part of the span; high half, its part of the span, its outside piece.
- `fn expose(t: *Tree, track: u32, value: [2]f32) void`: Writes the thumbs' value columns: each thumb's half holds its value.
- `fn semantics(t: *const Tree, s: *a11y.Semantics, id: Id, value: [2]f32, lo: f32, hi: f32) void`: Each thumb's range for assistive technology, for the range slider named `id` in `t` over `value` from `lo` to `hi`: the low thumb runs from `lo` to the high thumb's value, the high thumb from the low thumb's value to `hi` (WAI-ARIA multi-thumb slider), both stepped as the slider's keys are.
- `fn range(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, value: *[2]f32, lo: f32, hi: f32) Response`: A range slider named `id` over `value` (low, high), from `lo` at the left end to `hi` at the right.
- `fn Comptime(comptime name: [:0]const u8, comptime bind: []const u8, comptime r: slider_zig.Range) type`: The comptime range slider named `name` over field `bind`, running `r` (`Spec.widget`): the same rows as `range`, and the same input (`step`) applied to the comptime front end's state, a held thumb following the pointer across the track's last rect.

### `ct` (struct)

- `fn range(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime bind: @EnumLiteral(), comptime r: slider_zig.Range) Spec`: A range slider named `id` over the `[2]f32` field `bind`, running `range.lo` to `range.hi`: `ct.range(th, .band, .band, .{ .hi = 20_000 })`.

### `Comptime(..)` (generic struct)
The comptime range slider named `name` over field `bind`, running `r` (`Spec.widget`): the same rows as `range`, and the same input (`step`) applied to the comptime front end's state, a held thumb following the pointer across the track's last rect.

- `fn bindError(comptime State: type, comptime field: []const u8) ?[]const u8`
- `fn push(ui: anytype, comptime n: Spec, parent: u32, next: *u32, in: *const Input) void`
- `fn step(ui: anytype, comptime row: u32, event: anytype, in: *const Input) void`

### `Page` (struct)
The range slider's example page: a frequency band (20..200) under its label on a panel, in both front ends.

- `State = struct { band_text: []const u8 = "Frequency", band: [2]f32 = .{ 60, 140 } }`
- `spec = spec_zig.col(panel, .{ base.ct.label(th, .band_text), ct.range(th, .band, .band, .{ .lo = 20, .hi = 200 }) })`
- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, .{ .nodes = 16, .texts = 4, .customs = 1, .strings = 256, .depth = 8 })`
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/range.replay")`
- `golden_frames = .{ .{ "rest", 0 }, .{ "dragged", 2 }, .{ "keys", 6 } }`: Frames kept as golden images, by name: tools/golden/ref/widgets/range-<name>.png.
- `fn semantics(t: *const Tree, s: *a11y.Semantics, str: *Strings, values: [2]f32) void`: The page's accessibility beyond the tree, for either front end's tree `t` over the band `values`, whose names go to the frame's strings `str`: each thumb's range, and a name for each (`Semantics.label`), as one caption cannot tell the two apart.

### `Page.App` (struct)

Fields: `band`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

## `lcgf.widgets_chip` (src/widgets/chip.zig)

Chip: a short caption on a pill (a tag, a filter, a recipient), optionally with a button that asks to remove it.

### Top level

- `fn removeId(owner: Id) Id`: The id of the remove button of the chip named `owner`, in both front ends: the runtime one derives it with `Id.with`, the comptime one from the button's spec name `<chip>.remove`.
- `fn chipName(comptime remove: []const u8) []const u8`: The chip's name from its remove button's comptime name `<chip>.remove`.
- `fn chipStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The pill: caption (then the remove button) in a row, padded wider than tall, fully rounded at the theme's text size, in the control's hot colour so a remove button at rest shows on it; static text to assistive technology through its caption, nothing itself.
- `fn removeStyle(th: *const Theme, r: Response) Style`: The remove button: a small round button (role button) around its caption, filled from the control fills by hover and held as a button is (`button.buttonFill`).
- `fn chip(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, str: []const u8, remove: ?[]const u8) Response`: A chip showing `str`, with a remove button named `removeId(id)` and captioned `remove` when that is not null.
- `fn Comptime(comptime name: [:0]const u8, comptime removable: bool) type`: The comptime chip named `name`, with a remove button when `removable` (`Spec.widget`): the same rows as `chip`, the remove button with the comptime front end's hover and held, and Enter and Space on it while focused turned into its event, as `chip` sets `clicked`.

### `ct` (struct)

- `fn chip(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime text: anytype, comptime remove: anytype) Spec`: A chip named `id` showing the string field `text`, with a remove button captioned by the string field `remove` when it is not null: `ct.chip(th, .rust, .rust_text, .remove_text)` or `ct.chip(th, .draft, .draft_text, null)`.

### `Comptime(..)` (generic struct)
The comptime chip named `name`, with a remove button when `removable` (`Spec.widget`): the same rows as `chip`, the remove button with the comptime front end's hover and held, and Enter and Space on it while focused turned into its event, as `chip` sets `clicked`.

- `fn push(ui: anytype, comptime n: Spec, parent: u32, next: *u32, in: *const Input) void`
- `fn step(ui: anytype, comptime row: u32, event: anytype, in: *const Input) @TypeOf(event)`

### `Page` (struct)
The chip's example page: removable chips Zig and Rust and a plain Draft on a panel, in both front ends.

- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, .{ .nodes = 32, .texts = 8, .customs = 1, .strings = 256, .depth = 8 })`
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/chip.replay")`
- `golden_frames = .{ .{ "rest", 0 }, .{ "hover", 1 }, .{ "held", 2 } }`: Frames kept as golden images, by name: tools/golden/ref/widgets/chip-<name>.png.

### `Page.App` (struct)

Fields: `removed`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

### `Page.State` (struct)

Fields: `zig_text`, `rust_text`, `draft_text`, `remove_text`


## `lcgf.widgets_list` (src/widgets/list.zig)

List box (tier 2): a column of options, one of them selected, in both front ends.

### Top level

- `default_keys: Keymap = .{}`: The keys a list answers to.
- `fn check(comptime State: type, comptime selected: []const u8, comptime items: []const []const u8) ?[]const u8`: Why `State` cannot back a comptime list bound to `selected` with option fields `items`, or null when it can.
- `fn validate(comptime State: type, comptime selected: []const u8, comptime items: []const []const u8) void`: `check` as a compile error.
- `fn rowHeight(th: *const Theme) f32`: Height of every option row: one line of text and the theme's padding above and below.
- `fn inset(th: *const Theme) f32`: Inset of the option rows inside the list's border.
- `fn listStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`
- `fn listBorder(th: *const Theme, focused: bool) Color`: The list's outline by focus: at rest, focused.
- `fn optionStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`
- `fn optionFills(th: *const Theme) base.StateColors`: An option's fill by state, indexed by `hot | selected << 1`: rest, under the pointer, selected, selected and under the pointer.
- `fn optionFill(th: *const Theme, hot: bool, selected: bool) Color`
- `fn rowAt(th: *const Theme, top: f32, y: f32, count: u32) u32`: The option row at height `y`, for a list whose last rect starts at `top`: clamped into the `count` rows, so the border and padding pick the nearest row.
- `fn step(sel: u32, count: u32, code: u8, km: *const Keymap) u32`: The selection after one navigation key (WAI-ARIA listbox: selection follows focus).
- `fn typeAhead(items: []const []const u8, sel: u32, ch: u8) u32`: Type-ahead: the next option after `sel`, wrapping, whose text starts with `ch` (ASCII case ignored); `sel` when none does.
- `fn keys(sel: u32, items: []const []const u8, in: *const Input, km: *const Keymap) u32`
- `fn click(sel: u32, clicked: bool, row: u32) u32`: What a click does: selects the row under the pointer.
- `fn selectedValue(sel: u32) f32`: The `value` column of a list: the selected row, -1 for none.
- `fn setRoles(t: *Tree, box: u32, first_option: u32, count: u32) void`: Roles of the list's rows, written to the tree's `role` column (they are past `types.Role`): the list a list box, each option row an option.
- `fn setStates(s: *a11y.Semantics, first_option: u32, count: u32, sel: u32) void`: The `selected` state of the selected option, for the accessibility bridge.
- `fn list(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, items: []const []const u8, selected: *u32) Response`: A list of `items` with `selected.*` the selected row.
- `fn listFace(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, items: []const []const u8, selected: *u32, face: Style, text: types.TextStyle) Response`: `list` with its box laid out by `face` instead of `listStyle(th)` and its options' text drawn in `text`; focus still sets the border (`listBorder`) and the state the options' fill.
- `fn itemFields(comptime n: Spec) [n.children.len][]const u8`: The option fields of a fully bound list node.
- `fn ctResponse(ui: anytype, comptime row: u32, in: *const Input) Response`: Hover, held and focus of the widget at spec row `row` of a `base.ComptimeUi` page, as `Frame.interact` returns them (`ComptimeUi.response` plus its `focus`).
- `fn ctClicked(ui: anytype, comptime row: u32, event: anytype) bool`: Whether this frame's event is a click on the widget at spec row `row`.
- `fn ctRect(ui: anytype, comptime row: u32) types.Rect`: Last frame's rect of the widget at spec row `row`.
- `fn widgetRow(comptime Ui: type, comptime id: []const u8) u32`: The spec row of the widget named `id` on page `Ui`: its `Widget` tag's value.
- `fn pushLabel(tb: anytype, parent: u32, style: *const Style, str: []const u8, ts: *const types.TextStyle) void`: A text leaf as `base.label` makes it: `style` (a label's), the string, its text style `ts` (`base.labelText` for the theme's).
- `fn labelCount(comptime n: Spec) u32`: Spec nodes in `n`'s subtree that show text.
- `fn nodeAt(comptime n: Spec, comptime row: comptime_int) Spec`: The spec node at depth-first row `row` of `n`.
- `fn Example(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a caption and a list of five fruits on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `ct` (struct)

- `fn list(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime selected: @EnumLiteral(), comptime items: anytype) Spec`: A list named `id` selecting into the u32 field `selected`, one option per literal or bound caption in `items`: `ct.list(th, .fruit, .selected, .{ "Apple", .pear })`.

### `Example(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a caption and a list of five fruits on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `limits: context.Limits = .{ .nodes = 32, .texts = 16, .customs = 1, .strings = 1024, .depth = 8 }`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, limits)`

### `Example(..).App` (struct)
Runtime front end: the application owns the options and the selection.

Fields: `selected`, `clicked`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

### `Example(..).State` (struct)
Comptime front end: the same page as a spec over a state struct.

Fields: `title`, `selected`, `apple`, `banana`, `cherry`, `damson`, `elder`


### `Page` (struct)
The example page in the default theme, its replay and the frames kept as golden images (tools/golden/ref/widgets/list-<name>.png), as tools/golden/widgets.zig takes a page.

- `Run = Example(.default).Run`
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/list.replay")`

## `lcgf.widgets_vlist` (src/widgets/vlist.zig)

Virtual list (tier 2): a list box over any number of options that builds only the rows on screen (docs/TARGETS.md E1: cost scales with the screen, not with the data).

### Top level

- `Window = struct { first: u32, len: u32 }`: The rows on screen: the first one and how many, for `count` options scrolled by `scroll`.
- `fn check(comptime State: type, comptime items: []const u8, comptime selected: []const u8, comptime scroll: []const u8, comptime visible: u32) ?[]const u8`: Why `State` cannot back a comptime virtual list with options field `items`, selection field `selected` and scroll field `scroll`, showing `visible` rows; null when it can.
- `fn validate(comptime State: type, comptime items: []const u8, comptime selected: []const u8, comptime scroll: []const u8, comptime visible: u32) void`: `check` as a compile error.
- `fn capacity(visible: u32) u32`: Rows built for a list showing `visible` rows: one more, for the row half scrolled in at the bottom while another is half scrolled out at the top.
- `fn window(th: *const Theme, count: u32, visible: u32, scroll: f32) Window`
- `fn vlistStyle(th: *const Theme, visible: u32, scroll: f32) Style`: The list box's look (`list.listStyle`), `visible` rows tall, clipped, scrolled by the part of `scroll` that the first built row does not take up.
- `fn rowAt(th: *const Theme, top: f32, y: f32, scroll: f32, count: u32) u32`: The option row at height `y`, for a list whose last rect starts at `top` scrolled by `scroll`; clamped into the `count` rows.
- `fn maxScroll(th: *const Theme, count: u32, visible: u32) f32`: The largest scroll offset: the last row at the bottom edge.
- `fn wheel(th: *const Theme, scroll: f32, delta: f32, count: u32, visible: u32) f32`: The scroll offset after a wheel or touchpad movement of `delta` pixels, clamped.
- `fn reveal(th: *const Theme, scroll: f32, sel: u32, visible: u32) f32`: The scroll offset that shows row `sel` whole, moving as little as possible from `scroll`.
- `fn update(th: *const Theme, r: Response, top: f32, options: []const []const u8, visible: u32, sel: *u32, scroll: *f32, in: *const Input) void`: One frame of input, as `list`'s plus scrolling: a click selects the row under the pointer, keys move the selection while focused (`list.keys`), a changed selection is scrolled into view, then the wheel scrolls while the pointer is over the list.
- `fn setRoles(t: *Tree, box: u32, first_row: u32, len: u32) void`
- `fn vlist(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, options: []const []const u8, visible: u32, selected: *u32, scroll: *f32) Response`: A virtual list of `options` showing `visible` rows: as `list.list`, plus the wheel scrolls it, and only the rows on screen are built.
- `fn Example(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a caption and a virtual list of 200 options, five rows tall, on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `ct` (struct)

- `fn vlist(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime items: @EnumLiteral(), comptime selected: @EnumLiteral(), comptime scroll: @EnumLiteral(),…`: A virtual list named `id` over the options field `items` ([]const []const u8), selecting into the u32 field `selected`, scrolled by the f32 field `scroll`, `visible` rows tall.
- `fn fields(comptime n: Spec) struct { items: []const u8, selected: []const u8, scroll: []const u8, visible: u32 }`: The fields and row count a `ct.vlist` node was built with.

### `Example(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a caption and a virtual list of 200 options, five rows tall, on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `visible = 5`
- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `limits: context.Limits = .{ .nodes = 32, .texts = 16, .customs = 1, .strings = 1024, .depth = 8 }`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, limits)`

### `Example(..).App` (struct)
Runtime front end: the application owns the options, selection and scroll offset.

Fields: `options`, `selected`, `scroll`, `clicked`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

### `Example(..).State` (struct)
Comptime front end: the same page as a spec over a state struct.

Fields: `title`, `options`, `selected`, `scroll`


### `Page` (struct)
The example page in the default theme, its replay and the frames kept as golden images (tools/golden/ref/widgets/vlist-<name>.png), as tools/golden/widgets.zig takes a page.

- `Run = Example(.default).Run`
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/vlist.replay")`

## `lcgf.widgets_table` (src/widgets/table.zig)

Table (tier 2): a grid of text cells under a row of column headers, in both front ends.

### Top level

- `handle_px: f32 = 4`: Width of a resize handle at a header's right edge.
- `min_width: f32 = 24`: Narrowest and widest a column can be dragged.
- `max_width: f32 = 400`
- `fn State(comptime cols: usize) type`: What a table keeps between frames, owned by the application: the active cell (as shown, after sorting), the sort column (`none` for data order) and direction, and each column's width in logical pixels.
- `fn check(comptime S: type, comptime state: []const u8, comptime headers: []const u8, comptime cells: []const u8, comptime cols: u32, comptime rows: u32) ?[]c…`: Why `S` cannot back a comptime table bound to the state field `state`, with header strings in `headers` and cell strings in `cells`, `cols` columns by `rows` rows; null when it can.
- `fn validate(comptime S: type, comptime state: []const u8, comptime headers: []const u8, comptime cells: []const u8, comptime cols: u32, comptime rows: u32) v…`: `check` as a compile error.
- `fn headerId(id: Id, k: usize) Id`: Ids of a table's parts, all derived from the table's id: the body is the table's id itself, header `k` and its resize handle are numbered after it.
- `fn handleId(id: Id, cols: usize, k: usize) Id`
- `fn rowHeight(th: *const Theme) f32`: Height of the header row and of every body row: the list box's row height.
- `fn tableStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`
- `fn headerRowStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`
- `fn headerStyle(th: *const Theme, width: f32) Style`
- `fn headerFill(th: *const Theme, r: Response) Color`: A header's fill by state, indexed by `hovered | held << 1` (`base.stateIndex`).
- `fn markStyle(th: *const Theme, width: f32, sorted: bool, descending: bool) Style`: The sort mark in a header: a dot before the handle, the accent ascending, the text colour descending, nothing when the column is not the sort column.
- `fn handleStyle(th: *const Theme, width: f32, r: Response) Style`: The resize handle, flush with the header's right edge; lit while under the pointer or held.
- `fn bodyStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`
- `fn rowStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`
- `fn cellStyle(th: *const Theme, width: f32, active: bool, focused: bool) Style`: A body cell; the active one is lit, in the accent while the body has focus.
- `fn colAt(widths: []const f32, left: f32, x: f32) u32`: The column at `x`, for a body whose last rect starts at `left`: clamped into the columns.
- `fn rowAt(th: *const Theme, top: f32, y: f32, rows: u32) u32`: The body row at `y`, for a body whose last rect starts at `top`: clamped into `rows`.
- `fn order(cells: []const []const u8, cols: usize, sort: u32, descending: bool, out: []u32) void`
- `fn sortBy(st: anytype, k: u32) void`: A click on header `k`: sort by it ascending, or flip the direction if it already sorts.
- `fn resize(width: f32, held: bool, x0: f32, x: f32) f32`: Column `k`'s width while its handle is held: the pointer's distance from the header's left edge `x0`, clamped.
- `fn keys(st: anytype, rows: u32, in: *const Input) void`: The active cell after this frame's key events (press and repeat), clamped at the edges: arrows move by one, Home and End go to the first and last column of the row.
- `fn update(st: anytype, th: *const Theme, rows: u32, headers: []const Response, handles: []const Response, header_x0: []const f32, body: Response, body_rect: …`: One frame of input for a table: header clicks sort, held handles resize, a body click sets the active cell, keys move it while the body is focused.
- `fn sortValue(sorted: bool, descending: bool) f32`: A header's `value`: its sort direction, 1 ascending, -1 descending, 0 when it does not sort (ARIA `aria-sort`).
- `fn setRoles(t: *Tree, r: Rows) void`: Roles past `types.Role`, written to the tree's `role` column: the table a grid, the header row a row of column headers, each resize handle a separator (its value is the width), the body a row group of rows of cells.
- `fn setStates(s: *a11y.Semantics, r: Rows, row: u32, col: u32) void`: The `selected` state of the active cell, for the bridge; run before `a11y.states`.
- `fn table(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, comptime cols: usize, headers: []const []const u8, cells: []const []const u8, st: *State(cols), order_buf: []u3…`: A table of `cols` columns: `headers` (one per column) above `cells` (row-major), with `st` its state and `order_buf` (one entry per row) the caller's scratch for the sort order.
- `fn Example(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a caption and a table of five files in three columns on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `State(..)` (generic struct)
What a table keeps between frames, owned by the application: the active cell (as shown, after sorting), the sort column (`none` for data order) and direction, and each column's width in logical pixels.

Fields: `row`, `col`, `sort`, `descending`, `widths`


### `Rows` (struct)
Rows of a table as both front ends build them, from the table's row `box`: what the accessibility passes and the tests need to find each part.

Fields: `box`, `cols`, `rows`

- `fn header(r: Rows, k: usize) u32`: A header cell is itself, its label, its sort mark and its handle.
- `fn handle(r: Rows, k: usize) u32`
- `fn body(r: Rows) u32`
- `fn row(r: Rows, i: usize) u32`: Row `i` of the body is itself and two rows (cell, label) per column.
- `fn cell(r: Rows, i: usize, k: usize) u32`

### `ct` (struct)

- `fn table(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime state: @EnumLiteral(), comptime headers: @EnumLiteral(), comptime cells: @EnumLiteral(), c…`: A table named `id` (its body; headers and handles are named after it), bound to the `State(cols)` field `table`, showing the strings fields `headers` (one per column) and `cells` (row-major), `cols` columns by `rows` rows.
- `fn fields(comptime n: Spec) struct { name: [:0]const u8, state: []const u8, headers: []const u8, cells: []const u8, cols: u32, rows: u32 }`: The fields and size a `ct.table` node was built with.

### `Example(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a caption and a table of five files in three columns on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(PageState, spec, th)`
- `limits: context.Limits = .{ .nodes = 64, .texts = 32, .customs = 1, .strings = 1024, .depth = 8 }`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, limits)`

### `Example(..).App` (struct)
Runtime front end: the application owns the strings, the table state and the sort order's scratch.

Fields: `table`, `order`, `clicked`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

### `Example(..).PageState` (struct)
Comptime front end: the same page as a spec over a state struct.

Fields: `title`, `headers`, `cells`, `table`


### `Page` (struct)
The example page in the default theme, its replay and the frames kept as golden images (tools/golden/ref/widgets/table-<name>.png), as tools/golden/widgets.zig takes a page.

- `Run = Example(.default).Run`
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/table.replay")`

## `lcgf.widgets_tree` (src/widgets/tree.zig)

Tree view (tier 2): an outline of items, each at a depth, whose parents expand and collapse, in both front ends.

### Top level

- `indent_px: f32 = 16`: Indent per level of depth.
- `mark_px: f32 = 8`: Size of the disclosure mark in front of a parent's label.
- `fn State(comptime n: usize) type`: What a tree view keeps between frames, owned by the application: the selected item (an index into the items) and, per item, whether it is expanded (meaningful for parents).
- `fn check(comptime S: type, comptime state: []const u8, comptime labels: []const u8, comptime depths: []const u8, comptime n: u32) ?[]const u8`: Why `S` cannot back a comptime tree view bound to the state field `state`, with item labels in `labels` and depths in `depths`, `n` items; null when it can.
- `fn validate(comptime S: type, comptime state: []const u8, comptime labels: []const u8, comptime depths: []const u8, comptime n: u32) void`: `check` as a compile error.
- `fn hasChildren(depths: []const u8, i: usize) bool`: Whether item `i` has children: the next item is deeper.
- `fn parentOf(depths: []const u8, i: u32) u32`: Item `i`'s parent, or `none` at depth 0: the nearest earlier item one level up.
- `fn visibleItems(depths: []const u8, expanded: []const bool, out: []u32) u32`: The items whose ancestors are all expanded, in order, into `out`; returns how many.
- `fn positionOf(visible: []const u32, item: u32) u32`: Position of item `item` among the visible items, or `none` when it is hidden.
- `fn treeStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`
- `fn rowStyle(th: *const Theme, depth: u8) Style`: An item's row: the list box's option row, indented by its depth.
- `fn markStyle(th: *const Theme, parent: bool, expanded: bool) Style`: The disclosure mark: a dot, the accent when expanded, the hot control colour when collapsed, nothing for an item without children.
- `fn rowAt(th: *const Theme, top: f32, y: f32, count: u32) u32`: The visible position at height `y`, for a tree view whose last rect starts at `top`, clamped into the `count` visible rows.
- `fn onMark(th: *const Theme, left: f32, x: f32, depth: u8) bool`: Whether `x` is on item `depth`'s disclosure mark (or before it), for a tree view whose last rect starts at `left`: a click there toggles instead of only selecting.
- `fn step(st: anytype, depths: []const u8, visible: []const u32, code: u8) void`: The state after one key (WAI-ARIA APG tree view): Down and Up move among the visible items, Home and End to the first and last; Right expands a collapsed parent, or moves into an expanded one's first child; Left collapses an expanded parent, or moves to the parent.
- `fn click(st: anytype, depths: []const u8, visible: []const u32, pos: u32, on_mark: bool) void`: A click on visible row `pos`: selects its item, and toggles it when the click is on its disclosure mark.
- `fn update(st: anytype, th: *const Theme, depths: []const u8, r: Response, rect: types.Rect, in: *const Input, scratch: []u32) void`: One frame of input: a click, then this frame's keys while focused (press and repeat).
- `fn setRoles(t: *Tree, box: u32, len: u32) void`: Roles of the built rows: the tree view a tree, each item row a tree item.
- `fn setStates(s: *a11y.Semantics, box: u32, depths: []const u8, st: anytype, visible: []const u32) void`: States of the built items for the bridge: parents are expandable and say whether they are expanded, the selected item is selected.
- `fn tree(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, comptime n: usize, labels: []const []const u8, depths: []const u8, st: *State(n)) Response`: A tree view of `n` items, `labels` and `depths` in depth-first order, with `st` its state.
- `fn treeFace(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, comptime n: usize, labels: []const []const u8, depths: []const u8, st: *State(n), face: Style, text: types.T…`: `tree` with its box laid out by `face` instead of `treeStyle(th)` and its items' text drawn in `text`; focus still sets the border (`list.listBorder`) and the state the rows' fill.
- `fn Example(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a caption and a tree view of eight items (two top-level folders, `src` expanded) on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `State(..)` (generic struct)
What a tree view keeps between frames, owned by the application: the selected item (an index into the items) and, per item, whether it is expanded (meaningful for parents).

Fields: `selected`, `expanded`


### `ct` (struct)

- `fn tree(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime state: @EnumLiteral(), comptime labels: @EnumLiteral(), comptime depths: @EnumLiteral(), co…`: A tree view named `id` bound to the `State(n)` field `state`, over the `n` items of the fields `labels` ([]const []const u8) and `depths` ([]const u8).
- `fn fields(comptime n: Spec) struct { state: []const u8, labels: []const u8, depths: []const u8, n: u32 }`: The fields and item count a `ct.tree` node was built with.

### `Example(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a caption and a tree view of eight items (two top-level folders, `src` expanded) on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(PageState, spec, th)`
- `limits: context.Limits = .{ .nodes = 64, .texts = 16, .customs = 1, .strings = 1024, .depth = 8 }`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, limits)`

### `Example(..).App` (struct)
Runtime front end: the application owns the items and the tree state.

Fields: `outline`, `clicked`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

### `Example(..).PageState` (struct)
Comptime front end: the same page as a spec over a state struct.

Fields: `title`, `labels`, `depths`, `outline`


### `Page` (struct)
The example page in the default theme, its replay and the frames kept as golden images (tools/golden/ref/widgets/tree-<name>.png), as tools/golden/widgets.zig takes a page.

## `lcgf.widgets_progress` (src/widgets/progress.zig)

Progress bar (tier 2): how far a task has come, as a filled part of a track, in both front ends.

### Top level

- `fn check(comptime State: type, comptime value: []const u8) ?[]const u8`: Why `State` cannot back a comptime progress bar showing the field `value`; null when it can.
- `fn validate(comptime State: type, comptime value: []const u8) void`: `check` as a compile error.
- `fn trackStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The track: the full width it is given (at least 80 px), 8 px tall, rounded.
- `fn barStyle(th: *const Theme, value: f32) Style`: The filled part: `value` of the track's width, clamped to 0..1, in the accent.
- `fn progressValue(value: f32) f32`: The track's `value` column: the fraction done, clamped (aria-valuenow over 0..1).
- `fn setRole(t: *Tree, track: u32) void`: The track is a progress bar (`a11y.Role.progress_bar`, past `types.Role`).
- `fn setName(s: *a11y.Semantics, track: u32, label: u32) void`: Names the bar after the text of `label` (a caption's row), as `aria-labelledby`: a progress bar does not take its name from its content.
- `fn progress(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, value: f32) Node`: A progress bar showing `value` (0 to 1).
- `fn progressFace(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, value: f32, face: Style) Node`: `progress` with its track laid out by `face` instead of `trackStyle(th)`, as `spec.styled` restyles `ct.progress` (CSS on a `<progress>`); the bar is the theme's.
- `fn Example(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: two captioned progress bars on a panel (a download 40% done, an install finished), in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `fn progress(comptime th: Theme, comptime value: @EnumLiteral()) Spec`: A progress bar showing the f32 field `value`.

## `lcgf.widgets_spinner` (src/widgets/spinner.zig)

Spinner (tier 2): an indeterminate progress indicator, a ring of dots whose bright head goes round while the application is busy, in both front ends.

### Top level

- `dots = 8`: Dots in the ring.
- `steps_per_second = 12`: Steps the head takes per second.
- `size_px: f32 = 24`: Side of the spinner's square, and of each dot.
- `dot_px: f32 = 4`
- `fn check(comptime State: type, comptime busy: []const u8) ?[]const u8`: Why `State` cannot back a comptime spinner shown while the field `busy` is set; null when it can.
- `fn validate(comptime State: type, comptime busy: []const u8) void`: `check` as a compile error.
- `fn phase(time: f64) u32`: The dot the head is on at `time` seconds.
- `fn spinnerStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`
- `fn dotFill(th: *const Theme, k: u32, head: u32, busy: bool) Color`: Dot `k`'s fill at head position `head`: the accent, fading with each step behind the head, and nothing while not busy.
- `fn dotStyle(th: *const Theme, k: u32, head: u32, busy: bool) Style`
- `fn spinnerValue(busy: bool) f32`: The spinner's `value` column: -1 while busy (an indeterminate progress bar has no current value), 0 when idle.
- `fn setRole(t: *Tree, node: u32) void`: The spinner is a progress bar (`a11y.Role.progress_bar`, past `types.Role`).
- `fn setName(s: *a11y.Semantics, node: u32, label: u32) void`: Names the spinner after the text of `label` (a caption's row), as `aria-labelledby`.
- `fn spinner(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, busy: bool) Node`: A spinner, turning while `busy`.
- `fn Example(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a busy spinner beside its caption on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

## `lcgf.widgets_colorpicker` (src/widgets/colorpicker.zig)

Colour picker (tier 2): a saturation and value area dragged in two dimensions, a hue slider and an opacity slider, in both front ends.

### Top level

- `Part = enum(u32) { area = 1, hue = 2, alpha = 3 }`: The picker's parts, each a widget: the area, the hue slider, the opacity slider.
- `area_size = [2]f32{ 160, 120 }`: Size of the saturation and value area; the sliders are as wide.
- `track_px: f32 = 12`: Height of a slider track.
- `key_step: f32 = 0.01`: Step of an arrow key, as a fraction of a part's range.
- `fn check(comptime State: type, comptime color: []const u8) ?[]const u8`: Why `State` cannot back a comptime colour picker bound to the field `color`; null when it can.
- `fn validate(comptime State: type, comptime color: []const u8) void`: `check` as a compile error.
- `fn partId(id: Id, part: Part) Id`: A part's id, derived from the picker's id the same way in both front ends.
- `fn pickerStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`
- `fn areaStyle(th: *const Theme, c: Hsva) Style`: The area: its fill is the pure hue; two gradient layers on it whiten to the left and darken to the bottom.
- `fn areaLayer(dark: bool) Style`: The area's layers: white to clear from the left, then clear to black from the top.
- `fn areaMarker(c: Hsva) Style`: The ring marking saturation (across) and value (up) in the area.
- `fn trackStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: A slider track as wide as the area.
- `fn hueSegment(k: u32) Style`: Hue segment `k` of the hue track: a gradient from one stop to the next.
- `fn alphaFill(c: Hsva) Style`: The opacity track: the colour from clear to opaque.
- `fn thumbStyle(frac: f32) Style`: A slider's thumb at `frac` of the track: a white bar a little taller than the track.
- `fn dragArea(c: Hsva, held: bool, rect: Rect, x: f32, y: f32) Hsva`: While the area is held, saturation and value follow the pointer inside `rect`: across is saturation, up is value (both clamped).
- `fn sliderKeys(value: f32, in: *const Input) f32`: A slider's value after this frame's keys (press and repeat): Right and Up add a step, Left and Down take one, Home and End go to the ends (APG slider).
- `fn update(c: Hsva, r: [3]Response, rects: [3]Rect, in: *const Input) Hsva`: One frame of input for a picker: each part follows the pointer while held (the area in two dimensions, the sliders across, as `base.slide`), and the focused part answers keys (the area: Left and Right move saturation, Up and Down value).
- `fn values(c: Hsva) [3]f32`: The parts' `value` columns: the area its saturation (a 2D slider has one value; its value is reported as saturation, ARIA has no two-axis slider), the hue slider degrees, the opacity slider its fraction.
- `fn groupValue(c: Hsva) f32`
- `fn setName(s: *a11y.Semantics, group: u32, label: u32) void`: Names the picker after the text of `label` (a caption's row), as `aria-labelledby`.
- `fn colorpicker(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, color: *Hsva) Response`: A colour picker for `color`.
- `fn Example(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a caption and a colour picker on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `Hsva` (struct)
A colour as the picker keeps it: hue, saturation, value and opacity, each 0 to 1.

Fields: `h`, `s`, `v`, `a`

- `fn rgba(c: Hsva) Color`: The colour in 8-bit RGBA (standard HSV to RGB, sector by sector).

### `ct` (struct)

- `fn colorpicker(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime color: @EnumLiteral()) Spec`: A colour picker named `id` for the `Hsva` field `color`.
- `fn fields(comptime n: Spec) struct { name: []const u8, color: []const u8 }`: The name and field a `ct.colorpicker` node was built with.

### `Example(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a caption and a colour picker on a panel, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `start: Hsva = .{ .h = 0.6, .s = 0.5, .v = 0.75, .a = 1 }`

- `limits: context.Limits = .{ .nodes = 32, .texts = 8, .customs = 1, .strings = 256, .depth = 8 }`

Fields: `color`

Card and group box (tier 2): a container that frames the widgets inside it under a title.

## `lcgf.widgets_separator` (src/widgets/separator.zig)

Separator (tier 2): a thin rule between groups of widgets, across its parent.

### Top level

- `Orientation = enum(u1) { horizontal, vertical }`: Which way the rule runs: a horizontal rule separates rows of a column, a vertical one the items of a row.
- `role: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.separator)`: What assistive technology sees: a `separator` (not focusable, so it has no value), with no name and no states.
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/separator.replay")`: Input for `Page(.default)` at 320 x 200, one commented line per frame.
- `fn separator(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, orientation: Orientation) Node`: A rule across the parent, `orientation` picking which way.
- `fn separatorFace(f: *Frame, face: Style) Node`: A rule laid out by `face` instead of `style(th, orientation)`, as `spec.styled` restyles `ct.separator` (CSS on an `<hr>`).
- `fn style(th: *const Theme, orientation: Orientation) Style`: The rule's node: it fills its parent along its length and is 1 px thick, painted in the theme's outline colour (`control_hot`, as a group box's frame and a checkbox's box).
- `fn Page(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: two labels split by a horizontal rule, then a row of two labels split by a vertical one, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `ct` (struct)
The separator as a spec node.

## `lcgf.theme` (src/theme/theme.zig)

Theme (tier 2): design tokens, and the widget theme they produce.

### Top level

- `role_count = @typeInfo(Role).@"enum".field_names.len`
- `Palette = [role_count]Color`: One colour per `Role`, indexed by `@intFromEnum(role)`.
- `Step = enum(u8) { xs, sm, md, lg, xl }`: A step on a size scale, smallest first.
- `step_count = @typeInfo(Step).@"enum".field_names.len`
- `Scale = [step_count]f32`: One size per `Step`, in logical pixels, non-decreasing.
- `dark: Tokens = .{}`: The prototype's theme: light text on dark surfaces.
- `light: Tokens = .{ .palette = light_palette }`: The same sizes in `light_palette`.
- `Density = enum(u8) { compact, comfortable, spacious }`: How tightly controls pack: a factor on the space scale.
- `States = [8]Look`: One `Look` per combination of interaction bits, indexed by `stateBits`: `hovered | held << 1 | focused << 2`.
- `sheen_wgsl = @embedFile("sheen.wgsl")`: An example custom material (sheen.wgsl): a themed surface lit from above.
- `sheen_shader = quad_shader.shader(sheen_wgsl)`: The full quad shader for the sheen material, ready for `Materials.add`.
- `Dark = Folded(dark, .comfortable)`
- `Light = Folded(light, .comfortable)`
- `palettes = [_]Palette{ dark_palette, light_palette }`: The stock palettes, by index: what a run-time theme switch picks from.
- `fn forAppearance(a: @import("../core/input.zig").Input.Appearance) Tokens`: The tokens that follow the platform's appearance (`Input.appearance`): `dark` or `light`, with the system's accent for checked boxes, slider fills and focus when it gives one.
- `fn dense(tok: Tokens, d: Density) Tokens`: `tok` at density `d`: each space step scaled and rounded to whole pixels, so edges stay on the pixel grid.
- `fn stateBits(r: Response) u3`: The row of a `States` table for response `r`.
- `fn controlStates(tok: *const Tokens) States`: A control's looks from the tokens: rest, hot, held (held looks held wherever the pointer is, as in the prototype's button), and the same four focused with an accent outline `xs` wide.
- `fn styled(s: Style, states: *const States, r: Response) Style`: `s` with the look of state `r` from `states`: one table load.
- `fn widgets(tok: *const Tokens) base.Theme`: The widget theme of `tok`: every widget reads its look from this, so it is the only place tokens meet widgets.
- `fn override(th: base.Theme, patch: anytype) base.Theme`: `th` with the fields of `patch` replaced: `override(th, .{ .accent = red, .pad = 12 })`.
- `fn sheen(s: Style, m: types.Material, strength: f32) Style`: `s` drawn with sheen material `m` (the handle `Materials.add(sheen_shader)` returned), lifted toward white by `strength`, 0 to 1, at its top edge.
- `fn Folded(comptime tok: Tokens, comptime d: Density) type`: Theme `tok` at density `d`, folded into constants: the widget theme and the state table are computed by the compiler and land in read-only data, as the comptime front end's flatten tables do (prototype/comptime_ui_probe.zig).
- `fn withPalette(tok: Tokens, p: Palette) Tokens`: `tok` with palette `p`: switching theme at run time is swapping the palette, sizes stay.
- `fn recolor(d: *DrawList, from: *const Palette, to: *const Palette) void`: Switches a frame painted in palette `from` to palette `to` without rebuilding it: each quad fill and outline and each text colour that is `from[role]` becomes `to[role]`.
- `fn distinct(p: *const Palette) bool`: Whether `p`'s colours are distinct, which `recolor` needs of its `from`.
- `fn inheriting(th: base.Theme) base.Theme`: `th` whose labels inherit their colour and size from the tree (`Inherited`) instead of carrying the theme's: every widget's text follows, with no per-widget code.
- `fn endInherited(f: *runtime_frame.Frame, font: Mono, in: Inherited) Output`: `Frame.end` with `in.resolve` before text is measured.
- `fn expectSwitch(comptime a: base.Theme, comptime b: base.Theme) !void`: The theme switch test: the reference page (`widgets_base.Reference`) driven in theme `a` and in theme `b` by the same script of hovers, clicks and drags, each aimed at last frame's rect of its target so any layout is hit.

### `Role` (enum)
What a colour is for.


### `Tokens` (struct)

Fields: `palette`, `space`, `radius`, `text_size`

- `fn color(tok: *const Tokens, role: Role) Color`
- `fn spaceAt(tok: *const Tokens, s: Step) f32`
- `fn radiusAt(tok: *const Tokens, s: Step) f32`
- `fn textAt(tok: *const Tokens, s: Step) f32`

### `Look` (struct)
What an interaction state changes on a control: its face and its outline.

Fields: `fill`, `border_color`, `border`


### `Folded(..)` (generic struct)
Theme `tok` at density `d`, folded into constants: the widget theme and the state table are computed by the compiler and land in read-only data, as the comptime front end's flatten tables do (prototype/comptime_ui_probe.zig).

- `tokens: Tokens = dense(tok, d)`
- `theme: base.Theme = widgets(&tokens)`
- `states: States = controlStates(&tokens)`
- `fn look(r: Response) Look`: The look of state `r`: one load from the folded table.

### `Inherited` (struct)
Text colour and size a node hands down to its subtree: two caller-owned columns, one row per tree row.

Fields: `color`, `px`

- `fn clear(in: Inherited, th: *const base.Theme) void`: Every row inherits, and the root starts from `th`'s text.
- `fn set(in: Inherited, node: types.Node, color: Color, px: f32) void`: Node `node`'s subtree takes colour `color` and size `px` (either 0 to keep inheriting).
- `fn resolve(in: Inherited, t: *const Tree, tx: *Texts) void`: Resolves the columns down `t` (rows are in push order, so a parent is final before its children read it), then gives each text run of `tx` that inherits (colour 0 or size 0) its node's resolved value.

## `lcgf.theme_table` (src/theme/table.zig)

Run-time theme tokens (docs/plans/schemify.md §2): a caller-owned table of the values a compiled page reads while it builds (lengths, font sizes, radii, spacing, colours), with a generation that every change bumps, so a page that saw another generation lays out again.

### Top level

- `fn ThemeTable(comptime Tokens: type) type`

### `ThemeTable(..)` (generic struct)

Fields: `values`, `generation`

- `Token = std.meta.FieldEnum(Tokens)`
- `fn set(t: *Self, comptime token: Token, value: @FieldType(Tokens, @tagName(token))) void`: Sets one token and bumps the generation.
- `fn changedSince(t: *const Self, seen: u32) bool`: Whether the table changed since a reader saw generation `seen`.

## `lcgf.theme_tests` (src/theme/tests.zig)

Theme tests across the widget set: every stock theme and density applied to every widget page that takes a theme, through both front ends.

## `lcgf.widgets_check` (src/widgets/check.zig)

Checks shared by the widget tests: that rows take no input, and what assistive technology reads of a row.

### Top level

- `fn expectInert(t: *const Tree, inert: []const u32) !void`: Checks that rows `inert` take no input in laid-out tree `t`: they sense nothing, a hit test at each one's centre finds something else (or nothing), and walking the tab order from nothing (`focus.seek`, what Tab does) never stops on one.
- `fn nameOf(sem: *const a11y.Semantics, tx: *const Texts, strings: []const u8, row: u32) []const u8`: The accessible name of row `row` as the bridge reads it: the bytes of its name run.

### `SemBuf` (struct)
Semantic columns for a test tree of up to 32 rows.

Fields: `buf`

- `fn init(b: *SemBuf) a11y.Semantics`

## `lcgf.widgets_spacer` (src/widgets/spacer.zig)

Spacer (tier 2): empty space that pushes its siblings apart, as a flexible gap in a row or column: `[Open] [Save] <spacer> [Close]` puts Close at the far end.

### Top level

- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/spacer.replay")`: Input for `Page(.default)` at 320 x 200, one commented line per frame.
- `fn spacer(f: *Frame, axis: Axis) Node`: Space along `axis`, the axis of the row (`.x`) or column (`.y`) it sits in.
- `fn style(axis: Axis) Style`: The spacer's node: it fills what is left along `axis` (`Size.fill`), so siblings after it go to the far end, and is 0 across, so it never widens its parent.
- `fn Page(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a toolbar row of two buttons, a spacer and a third button, and a column with a label, a spacer and a label at its foot, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `ct` (struct)
The spacer as a spec node.

- `fn spacer(comptime axis: Axis) Spec`: Space along `axis`.

### `Page(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a toolbar row of two buttons, a spacer and a third button, and a column with a label, a spacer and a label at its foot, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, .{ .nodes = 32, .texts = 8, .customs = 1, .strings = 256, .depth = 8 })`

### `Page(..).App` (struct)

Fields: `closes`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

## `lcgf.widgets_accordion` (src/widgets/accordion.zig)

Accordion (tier 2): sections stacked in a column, each a header that shows or hides the panel below it.

### Top level

- `enter = keys.enter`: The keys that activate a focused header: Enter and Space (src/widgets/keys.zig).
- `space = keys.space`
- `region: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.region)`: What assistive technology sees of a section (WAI-ARIA APG accordion pattern): the section is a `group`, its header a `button` named by its title (from content) whose value is 1 while expanded, and the panel a `region`.
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/accordion.replay")`: Input for `Page(.default)` at 320 x 200, one commented line per frame.
- `fn open(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, expanded: *bool, title: []const u8) Response`: Starts a section with header `title` and the identity `id`, showing its panel while `expanded.*` is set.
- `fn openFace(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, expanded: *bool, title: []const u8, section: Style, header: Style) Response`: `open` with the section laid out by `section` and the header by `header` instead of the theme's styles, as `ct.sectionFace` lays out the comptime one (CSS on a `<details>` and its `<summary>`); the header's fill still follows hover and held.
- `fn close(f: *Frame) void`: Ends the section `open` started.
- `fn sectionStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: A section spans its parent's width: the header above, the panel below.
- `fn headerStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The header: a row across the section holding the indicator and the title, padded and rounded as a button.
- `fn indicatorStyle(th: *const Theme, expanded: bool) Style`: The mark before the title: the accent colour while the section is open, the control colour while it is closed (`Theme.marks`, by table index).
- `fn panelStyle(th: *const Theme, expanded: bool) Style`: The panel: as tall as its children when expanded, 0 px and clipping when collapsed, picked by table index.
- `fn expand(expanded: bool, clicked: bool, focused: bool, in: *const Input) bool`: The section's next expanded state: a click on its header toggles it, and so does each Enter or Space while the header has focus (WAI-ARIA APG accordion pattern).
- `fn semantics(t: *const Tree, s: *a11y.Semantics, row: u32) void`: Fills the caller's semantic columns for the section at tree row `row`, from the tree: the header is expandable, and expanded while its value is 1; the panel and the section are labelled by the header.
- `fn Page(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: two sections, "General" (open) holding a checkbox and "Advanced" (closed) holding a slider, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `ct` (struct)
The accordion's sections as spec nodes.

- `fn section(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime expanded: @EnumLiteral(), comptime title: anytype, comptime children: anytype) Spec`: A section whose header is the widget `id`, titled by the string field `title`, showing `children` (a tuple of specs) while the bool field `expanded` is set.
- `fn sectionFace(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime expanded: @EnumLiteral(), comptime title: anytype, comptime section_style: Style, co…`: `section` laid out by `section_style` and `header` instead of the theme's styles (CSS on a `<details>` and its `<summary>`); the runtime twin is `openFace`.

### `Page(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: two sections, "General" (open) holding a checkbox and "Advanced" (closed) holding a slider, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, .{ .nodes = 32, .texts = 8, .customs = 1, .strings = 256, .depth = 8 })`

### `Page(..).App` (struct)

Fields: `general`, `advanced`, `wire`, `level`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

### `Page(..).State` (struct)

Fields: `general`, `general_text`, `advanced`, `advanced_text`, `wire`, `wire_text`, `level`


## `lcgf.widgets_tabs` (src/widgets/tabs.zig)

Tabs (tier 2): a row of tabs above a stack of panels, showing the panel of the selected tab.

### Top level

- `Move = struct { selected: u32, focus: u32 }`: Where selection and focus go: tab indices, `none` for "no tab focused".
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/tabs.replay")`: Input for `Page(.default)` at 320 x 200, one commented line per frame.
- `fn open(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, ids: []const Id, selected: *u32, titles: []const []const u8) void`: Starts the tab set and builds its tab list: tab `k` has the identity `ids[k]` and shows `titles[k]`.
- `fn openFace(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, ids: []const Id, selected: *u32, titles: []const []const u8, face: Style, text: types.TextStyle) void`: `open` with the tab set laid out by `face` instead of `setStyle(th)` and the titles drawn in `text`; the tabs' fill still follows the selection and the state (`tabFill`).
- `fn panel(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, selected: u32, k: u32) Node`: Starts the panel of tab `k`; build its children, then `f.close()`.
- `fn close(f: *Frame) void`: Ends the tab set `open` started.
- `fn setStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The tab set: the tab list above the panels.
- `fn listStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The tab list: the tabs side by side.
- `fn tabStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: One tab: its title, padded, with its top corners rounded so it joins the panels below.
- `fn tabFill(th: *const Theme, r: types.Response, selected: bool) types.Color`: A tab's fill: the selected tab takes the panels' colour, so it reads as the front of the stack; the others are filled as buttons, by interaction state.
- `fn panelsStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The stack of panels, on the panel colour and padded; its top-left corner is square, under the first tab.
- `fn panelStyle(th: *const Theme, shown: bool) Style`: One panel: as tall as its children while its tab is selected, else 0 px and clipping, picked by table index.
- `fn select(selected: u32, count: u32, clicked: u32, focused: u32, in: *const Input) Move`: The next selection and focus of a tab set of `count` tabs (WAI-ARIA APG tabs pattern, automatic activation).
- `fn semantics(t: *const Tree, s: *a11y.Semantics, row: u32, count: u32) void`: Fills the caller's semantic columns for the tab set at tree row `row` with `count` tabs, from the tree: the selected tab (the list's value) is `selected`, and each panel is labelled by its tab.
- `fn Page(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: three tabs, General (a checkbox), View (a slider) and About (a label), in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `role` (struct)
What assistive technology sees (WAI-ARIA APG tabs pattern): the tab list is a `tab_list` whose value is the selected index, each tab a `tab` named by its title (from content), each panel a `tab_panel`.

- `list: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.tab_list)`
- `tab: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.tab)`
- `panel: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.tab_panel)`

### `ct` (struct)
The tab set as a spec node.

- `fn tabs(comptime th: Theme, comptime selected: @EnumLiteral(), comptime tabs_: anytype) Spec`: Tabs over the u32 field `selected`.

### `Page(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: three tabs, General (a checkbox), View (a slider) and About (a label), in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `ids = [_]Id{ .from("general"), .from("view"), .from("about") }`
- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, .{ .nodes = 48, .texts = 12, .customs = 1, .strings = 256, .depth = 8 })`

### `Page(..).App` (struct)

Fields: `selected`, `wire`, `zoom`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

### `Page(..).State` (struct)

Fields: `selected`, `general_text`, `view_text`, `about_text`, `wire`, `wire_text`, `zoom`, `version`


## `lcgf.widgets_keys` (src/widgets/keys.zig)

Keys the widgets share: the scancodes that activate a focused widget and move inside a composite one, and how many activations a frame's key events hold.

### Top level

- `map: @import("../interact/focus.zig").Keymap = .{}`: The arrows, Home, End and Tab, as focus moves them (`focus.Keymap`).
- `enter: u8 = 0x28`
- `space: u8 = 0x2c`
- `page_up: u8 = 0x4b`
- `page_down: u8 = 0x4e`
- `right = map.right`: The arrows, Home and End by name, for widgets that read them themselves while focused.
- `left = map.left`
- `down = map.down`
- `up = map.up`
- `home = map.home`
- `end = map.end`
- `fn keyCount(in: *const Input, code: u8, kind: Input.Event.Kind) u32`: How many of this frame's key events are `kind` (`.press` counts auto-repeat too) for key `code` with no modifier held.
- `fn activations(in: *const Input) u32`: Presses of Enter or Space in this frame's key events (auto-repeat does not count).

## `lcgf.widgets_splitter` (src/widgets/splitter.zig)

Splitter (tier 2): two panes side by side (or one above the other) with a handle between them that moves the split.

### Top level

- `handle_px: f32 = 6`: How thick the handle is across the split, logical pixels.
- `cursors = [2]@import("../runtime/context.zig").Cursor{ .resize_h, .resize_v }`: The pointer over the handle shows which way it moves, by axis.
- `step: f32 = 0.05`: How far one arrow key moves the split.
- `role: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.separator)`: What assistive technology sees (WAI-ARIA APG window splitter pattern): the handle is a focusable `separator` whose value is the split, 0 to 1 (a bridge scales it to aria-valuenow's 0..100), labelled by the first pane.
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/splitter.replay")`: Input for `Page(.default)` at 320 x 200, one commented line per frame.
- `fn open(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, axis: Axis, split: *f32) void`: Starts a splitter along `axis` (`.x`: panes side by side) whose first pane takes `split.*` of it, and opens the first pane.
- `fn openFace(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, axis: Axis, split: *f32, face: Style) void`: `open` with the container laid out by `face` instead of `containerStyle(th, axis)`; its panes still run along `axis`.
- `fn handle(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, axis: Axis, split: f32) void`: Closes the first pane and opens the second: the handle, then the body that holds the second pane's children.
- `fn close(f: *Frame) void`: Closes the second pane and the splitter.
- `fn containerStyle(th: *const Theme, axis: Axis) Style`: The container: fills its parent, its panes laid along `axis`.
- `fn firstStyle(th: *const Theme, axis: Axis, split: f32) Style`: The first pane: `split` of the container along `axis`, the full container across.
- `fn handleStyle(th: *const Theme, axis: Axis) Style`: The handle: `handle_px` along `axis`, the full container across, with rounded ends.
- `fn handleFill(th: *const Theme, r: types.Response) types.Color`: The handle's fill by interaction state (`base.stateIndex`): control at rest, control-hot under the pointer, accent while dragged.
- `fn secondStyle(th: *const Theme, axis: Axis, split: f32) Style`: The second pane: the rest of the container, `1 - split`, holding the handle at its start and then the body.
- `fn bodyStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The body of the second pane: what the handle leaves of it.
- `fn along(box: types.Rect, axis: Axis, pointer: [2]f32) f32`: Where `pointer` is along `axis` of the container rect `box`, 0 at its start to 1 at its end (not clamped).
- `fn resize(split: f32, held: bool, at: f32, focused: bool, in: *const Input) f32`: The next split (WAI-ARIA APG window splitter pattern).
- `fn semantics(t: *const Tree, s: *a11y.Semantics, row: u32) void`: Fills the caller's semantic columns for the splitter whose container is tree row `row`: the handle is named by the first pane's text.
- `fn Page(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a side-by-side splitter at 30%, a label on the left and a checkbox on the right, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `ct` (struct)
The splitter as a spec node.

- `fn splitter(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime axis: Axis, comptime split: @EnumLiteral(), comptime first: anytype, comptime second: a…`: A splitter along `axis` over the f32 field `split`, its handle the widget `id`, its panes holding `first` and `second` (tuples of specs).

### `Page(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a side-by-side splitter at 30%, a label on the left and a checkbox on the right, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `spec = spec_zig.col(area, .{ct.splitter(th, .split_handle, .x, .split, .{base.ct.label(th, .files)}, .{base.ct.checkbox(th, .wire, .wire, .wire_text)})})`
- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, .{ .nodes = 32, .texts = 8, .customs = 1, .strings = 256, .depth = 8 })`

### `Page(..).App` (struct)

Fields: `split`, `wire`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

### `Page(..).State` (struct)

Fields: `split`, `files`, `wire`, `wire_text`


## `lcgf.widgets_dock` (src/widgets/dock.zig)

Dock (tier 2): a docking layout of up to five panes, a left, top, bottom and right pane around the centre, with a splitter between each side pane and the rest.

### Top level

- `Pane = enum(u3) { left, top, centre, bottom, right }`: A pane's place, in the order the panes are built.
- `Panes = struct { left: bool = true, top: bool = true, bottom: bool = true, right: bool = true }`: Which side panes exist; the centre always does.
- `f6: u8 = 0x3f`: The key that moves focus between panes (USB HID usage), as in most desktop editors.
- `fn paneId(id: Id, p: Pane) Id`: The id of pane `p` of dock `id`; its splitters are `id.with(1)` to `id.with(4)`.
- `fn rootStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The dock: fills its parent.
- `fn paneStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: A pane: fills its splitter's pane, its children in a column, clipped.
- `fn has(panes: Panes, p: Pane) bool`: Whether pane `p` exists in `panes`.
- `fn cycle(panes: Panes, from: ?Pane, step: i8) Pane`: The pane that focus moves to from `from` (or from no pane, `null`) by `step` (+1 F6, -1 Shift+F6), wrapping and skipping the panes `panes` lacks.
- `fn open(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, st: *State, panes: Panes) Dock`: Opens dock `id` with the side panes `panes`, sized by `st`; F6 and Shift+F6 in this frame's events move focus first.
- `fn openFace(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, st: *State, panes: Panes, face: Style) Dock`: `open` with the dock laid out by `face` instead of `rootStyle(th)`.
- `fn Comptime(comptime th: Theme, comptime name: [:0]const u8, comptime field: []const u8, comptime panes: Panes, comptime specs: [5]Spec, comptime firsts: [4]…`: The custom widget (`Spec.widget`) of `ct.dock`: its `step` moves the splits and F6 focus as `open` and the splitters do at run time; its `push` builds the rows they build, with the same ids.

### `State` (struct)
The sizes, as the four splitters' fractions (`splitter.open`'s `split`), in the caller's memory, one per dock id.

Fields: `splits`


### `Dock` (struct)
A dock being built: which pane comes next.

Fields: `th`, `id`, `st`, `panes`, `next`, `in_pane`

- `fn pane(d: *Dock, f: *Frame, p: Pane) void`: Ends the pane being built, if any, and opens pane `p`, which must exist and come after it; build its children next.
- `fn close(d: *Dock, f: *Frame) void`: Ends the last pane and the dock.

### `ct` (struct)

- `fn dock(comptime th: Theme, comptime name: @EnumLiteral(), comptime field: @EnumLiteral(), comptime panes: Panes, comptime children: anytype) Spec`: Dock `name` with the side panes `panes`, sized by the `State` field `field`; `children` holds one tuple of spec nodes per pane that exists, in `Pane` order (left, top, centre, bottom, right).

### `Comptime(..)` (generic struct)
The custom widget (`Spec.widget`) of `ct.dock`: its `step` moves the splits and F6 focus as `open` and the splitters do at run time; its `push` builds the rows they build, with the same ids.

- `fn bindError(comptime S: type, comptime bound: []const u8) ?[]const u8`
- `fn step(ui: anytype, comptime row: u32, event: anytype, in: *const Input) void`
- `fn push(ui: anytype, comptime n: Spec, parent: u32, next: *u32, in: *const Input) void`

## `lcgf.widgets_records` (src/widgets/records.zig)

Records (tier 2): a frame built from a node table, as an application that keeps its UI as data (Schemify's `ui.records`) builds it every frame through the runtime front end.

### Top level

- `kind_count = @typeInfo(Kind).@"enum".field_names.len`
- `Look = struct { style: Style = .{}, text: TextStyle = .{} }`: One look: a node's style and, where it draws text, the text's.
- `Field = textfield.Text(32)`: The text field each `field` row edits.
- `TreeState = tree.State(tree_labels.len)`: The tree view each `tree` row shows: `tree_labels` at `tree_depths`.
- `list_items = [_][]const u8{ "Resistor", "Capacitor", "Inductor", "Diode" }`
- `tree_labels = [_][]const u8{ "top", "amp", "bias", "out" }`
- `tree_depths = [_]u8{ 0, 1, 2, 1 }`
- `radio_captions = [_][]const u8{ "DC", "AC", "Transient" }`
- `tool_titles = [_][]const u8{ "Wire", "Probe", "Run" }`
- `menu_def: menu.Def = .{ .items = &.{ "copy", "paste", "delete" } }`
- `menu_texts = [_][]const u8{ "Copy", "Paste", "Delete" }`
- `bar_def: menubar.BarDef = .{ .names = &.{ "file", "edit" }, .menus = &.{ menu.file_menu, menubar.edit_menu } }`
- `bar_texts = [_][]const u8{ "File", "Edit" }`
- `bar_items = [_][]const []const u8{ &menu.file_texts, &.{ "Undo", "Redo" } }`
- `max_depth = 32`: The deepest nesting `build` follows.
- `fn kindLooks(th: *const Theme) [kind_count]Look`: Each kind's look in theme `th`: the style and text its theme function uses.
- `fn build(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, t: Table, looks: []const Look, st: State) void`: Builds the table's rows into `f`, at the depth `f` is open at (the page's root, so the overlays float over everything): one pass over the rows, then one over the overlays.
- `fn SceneState(comptime n: usize) type`: State for `scene`, in static memory (the scene's rows, at their defaults; splits at half).

### `Kind` (enum)
What a row builds.

- `fn container(k: Kind) bool`: Whether rows deeper than this one are its children.

### `Table` (struct)
The node table, one row per node in depth-first order.

Fields: `kind`, `depth`, `text`, `look`

- `fn len(t: Table) u32`
- `fn subtreeEnd(t: Table, r: u32) u32`: The row after the last of row `r`'s descendants.

### `State` (struct)
Per-row state, each column as long as the table (a row uses the columns its kind reads): `on` for a toggle, checkbox, switch, dialog or popup; `value` for a slider, progress bar or split; `sel` for a list, radio group, tab set or toolbar.

Fields: `on`, `value`, `sel`, `fields`, `trees`, `docks`, `menu`, `bar`


### `SceneState(..)` (generic struct)
State for `scene`, in static memory (the scene's rows, at their defaults; splits at half).

Fields: `on`, `value`, `sel`, `fields`, `trees`, `docks`, `menu`, `bar`

- `fn state(s: *@This()) State`

## `lcgf.widgets_scroll` (src/widgets/scroll.zig)

Scroll area (tier 2): a viewport that clips its content and shows the part at a vertical offset.

### Top level

- `bar_px: f32 = 8`: How wide the scroll bar is, logical pixels.
- `line_px: f32 = 40`: How far one wheel notch or one arrow key scrolls, logical pixels.
- `role: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.scroll_bar)`: What assistive technology sees.
- `view_role: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.scroll_view)`: The viewport's role byte.
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/scroll.replay")`: Input for `Page(.default)` at 320 x 200, one commented line per frame.
- `fn clamp(offset: f32, g: Geometry) f32`: `offset` kept inside 0..`g.limit()`.
- `fn open(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, offset: *f32) void`: Starts a scroll area named `id` showing its content from `offset.*` down, and opens the content.
- `fn close(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, id: Id, offset: f32) void`: Ends the scroll area `open` started.
- `fn areaStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The area: fills its parent; the viewport, then (C0597) the scroll bar beside it.
- `fn viewportStyle(th: *const Theme, offset: f32) Style`: The viewport: fills the area, clips, and shifts its content up by `offset`.
- `fn contentStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The content: as wide as the viewport, as tall as what it holds.
- `fn trackStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The scroll bar's track: beside the viewport, as tall as it, rounded.
- `fn thumbStyle(th: *const Theme, g: Geometry, offset: f32) Style`: The thumb: the viewport's share of the content, at the offset's share, floating in the track (whose height is the viewport's).
- `fn inside(r: Rect, p: [2]f32) bool`: Whether `p` is inside `r` (half-open, as hit testing).
- `fn scrolled(offset: f32, g: Geometry, over: bool, held: bool, dy: f32, focused: bool, in: *const Input) f32`: The next offset, clamped to `g`.
- `fn scrollerOf(t: *const @import("../core/tree.zig").Tree, row: u32) u32`: The viewport of the nearest scroll area at or above tree row `row`, or the root (0): the scroller a sticky node (`scroll.Sticky`, `spec.sticky`) sticks in.
- `fn position(offset: f32, g: Geometry) f32`: How far through the content `offset` is, 0 to 1; 0 when the content fits.
- `fn Page(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a 200 x 100 scroll area over a column of six labels and a checkbox, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `Geometry` (struct)
What last frame's layout says about a scroll area: how tall its viewport and its content were.

Fields: `view`, `content`

- `fn of(viewport: Rect, content: Rect) Geometry`: From last frame's viewport and content rects.
- `fn limit(g: Geometry) f32`: The largest offset: the content's overflow, 0 when it fits.

### `ct` (struct)
The scroll area as a spec node.

- `fn scroll(comptime th: Theme, comptime id: @EnumLiteral(), comptime offset: @EnumLiteral(), comptime children: anytype) Spec`: A scroll area whose viewport is the widget `id`, scrolled by the f32 field `offset`, holding `children` (a tuple of specs).

### `Page(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a 200 x 100 scroll area over a column of six labels and a checkbox, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, .{ .nodes = 32, .texts = 12, .customs = 1, .strings = 256, .depth = 10 })`

## `lcgf.widgets_toolbar` (src/widgets/toolbar.zig)

Toolbar (tier 2): a row of buttons that is one stop in the tab order.

### Top level

- `role: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.toolbar)`: What assistive technology sees (WAI-ARIA APG toolbar pattern): the bar is a `toolbar` holding `button`s named by their titles (from content).
- `Move = struct { current: u32, focus: u32, activated: u32 }`: Where the tab stop, focus and activation go: button indices, `none` for none.
- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/toolbar.replay")`: Input for `Page(.default)` at 320 x 200, one commented line per frame.
- `fn bar(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, ids: []const Id, titles: []const []const u8, current: *u32) u32`: A toolbar of one button per title: button `k` has the identity `ids[k]` and shows `titles[k]`.
- `fn barFace(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, ids: []const Id, titles: []const []const u8, current: *u32, face: Style, text: types.TextStyle) u32`: `bar` laid out by `face` instead of `barStyle(th)`, its buttons' titles drawn in `text`; the buttons' fill still follows their state.
- `fn barStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The bar: its buttons side by side on the panel colour, padded and spaced by the theme, as the reference page's toolbar.
- `fn itemStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: One button, as `base.buttonStyle`; filled as a button by interaction state (`base.buttonFill`).
- `fn sense(is_current: bool) types.Sense`: Only the current button senses focus, so the toolbar is one stop in the tab order (and in `focus.seek`); the others still sense the pointer.
- `fn rove(current: u32, count: u32, clicked: u32, focused: u32, in: *const Input) Move`: The next state of a toolbar of `count` buttons (WAI-ARIA APG toolbar pattern, roving tab stop).
- `fn Page(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a toolbar of Bold, Italic and Underline above a checkbox, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `ct` (struct)
The toolbar as a spec node.

- `fn toolbar(comptime th: Theme, comptime current: @EnumLiteral(), comptime activated: @EnumLiteral(), comptime items: anytype) Spec`: A toolbar whose tab stop is the u32 field `current` and which writes the button it activates each frame to the u32 field `activated` (`none` for none).

### `Page(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a toolbar of Bold, Italic and Underline above a checkbox, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `ids = [_]Id{ .from("bold"), .from("italic"), .from("under") }`
- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, .{ .nodes = 32, .texts = 8, .customs = 1, .strings = 256, .depth = 8 })`

### `Page(..).App` (struct)

Fields: `current`, `activated`, `wire`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

Fields: `current`, `activated`, `bold_text`, `italic_text`, `under_text`, `wire`, `wire_text`


## `lcgf.widgets_statusbar` (src/widgets/statusbar.zig)

Status bar (tier 2): a strip along the foot of a window showing short pieces of status text side by side ("Ready", "Ln 4, Col 12").

### Top level

- `role: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.status)`: What assistive technology sees: the strip is a `status`, a polite live region, so a screen reader announces its texts when they change without moving focus.

- `fn barFace(f: *Frame, texts: []const []const u8, face: Style, text: types.TextStyle) Node`: `bar` laid out by `face` instead of `barStyle(th)`, its texts drawn in `text`.
- `fn barStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The strip: as wide as its parent, on the panel colour, its texts in a row spaced by two pads, inset by a pad at the ends and by the gap above and below (a status bar is thinner than a toolbar).
- `fn Page(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a window whose body (a Save button) fills it, and a status bar of two texts at its foot, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `fn statusbar(comptime th: Theme, comptime texts: anytype) Spec`: A status bar showing the string fields `texts` (a tuple of field names) side by side.

## `lcgf.widgets_breadcrumb` (src/widgets/breadcrumb.zig)

Breadcrumb (tier 2): the trail of pages from the top down to the current one, each earlier page a link back to it: `Home › Docs › Widgets`.

### Top level

- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/breadcrumb.replay")`: Input for `Page(.default)` at 320 x 200, one commented line per frame.
- `fn trail(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, ids: []const Id, titles: []const []const u8) u32`: A trail of links `titles[0..n-1]` (link `k` has the identity `ids[k]`), each followed by a divider, then the current page `titles[n-1]`.
- `fn navStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The trail: its links and dividers in a row, spaced by the theme's gap.
- `fn linkStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: A link: its title, slightly padded and rounded so its hover fill reads as a target.
- `fn linkFill(th: *const Theme, r: types.Response) types.Color`: A link's fill by interaction state (`base.stateIndex`): none at rest, control-hot under the pointer, control-held while pressed.
- `fn textStyle() Style`: A title's text node, as `Frame.text` makes it.
- `fn linkText(th: *const Theme) TextStyle`: A link's title: the accent colour, as links are.
- `fn currentText(th: *const Theme) TextStyle`: The current page's title.
- `fn dividerStyle(th: *const Theme) Style`: The mark between two titles: a small dot in the outline colour.
- `fn semantics(t: *const Tree, s: *a11y.Semantics, first_link: Id) void`: Marks the current page (the trail's last item) `current` for assistive technology, in the trail whose first link is `first_link` in `t`.
- `fn follow(clicked: u32, focused: u32, in: *const Input) u32`: The link followed this frame (WAI-ARIA APG breadcrumb and link patterns): the one clicked, else the focused one if Enter was pressed (links take Enter, not Space), else `none`.
- `fn Page(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a trail Home › Docs › Widgets above a checkbox, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `role` (struct)
What assistive technology sees (WAI-ARIA APG breadcrumb pattern): the trail is a `navigation` landmark, each earlier page a `link` named by its title (from content), and the current page static text.

- `nav: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.navigation)`
- `link: u8 = @backingInt(a11y.Role.link)`

### `ct` (struct)
The breadcrumb as a spec node.

- `fn trail(comptime th: Theme, comptime activated: @EnumLiteral(), comptime links: anytype, comptime current: @EnumLiteral()) Spec`: A trail of links `links` (a tuple of `.{ id, title }`: the link's widget name and the string field it shows) ending at the string field `current`; the link activated each frame goes to the u32 field `activated` (`none` for none).

### `Page(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a trail Home › Docs › Widgets above a checkbox, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `ids = [_]Id{ .from("home"), .from("docs") }`
- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
- `Run = base.Harness(App, Ui, .{ .nodes = 32, .texts = 8, .customs = 1, .strings = 256, .depth = 8 })`

### `Page(..).App` (struct)

Fields: `activated`, `wire`

- `fn build(app: *App, f: *Frame) void`

### `Page(..).State` (struct)

Fields: `activated`, `home_text`, `docs_text`, `here`, `wire`, `wire_text`


## `lcgf.widgets_icon` (src/widgets/icon.zig)

Icon (tier 2): a small picture from a texture, sized in logical pixels, as DVUI draws its TinyVG icons.

### Top level

- `replay = @embedFile("testdata/icon.replay")`: Input for `Page(.default)` at 320 x 200, one commented line per frame.
- `fn icon(f: *Frame, th: *const Theme, ic: Icon) Node`: Draws `ic` as one leaf of its size.
- `fn iconFace(f: *Frame, face: Style) Node`: An icon laid out by `face` instead of `style(th, ic)`: its size, texture, uv and tint.
- `fn style(th: *const Theme, ic: Icon) Style`: The icon's node: exactly its size, one quad sampling `ic.texture` over `ic.uv`, tinted with the theme's text colour, as a glyph is: a coverage icon takes the colour of the text beside it.
- `fn name(s: *a11y.Semantics, row: u32, by: u32) void`: Names the icon at tree row `row` by the text of row `by` (`aria-labelledby`) in the caller's semantic columns.
- `fn Page(comptime th: Theme) type`: The example page: a 16 px icon before a label, and a 24 px icon inside a button, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

### `Icon` (struct)
Where a rasterised icon is: its texture, the rect it covers there (u0, v0, u1, v1), and the size it is drawn at, logical pixels.

Fields: `texture`, `uv`, `size`


### `ct` (struct)
The icon as a spec node.

- `fn icon(comptime th: Theme, comptime ic: Icon) Spec`: Draws `ic`.

### `Page(..)` (generic struct)
The example page: a 16 px icon before a label, and a 24 px icon inside a button, in both front ends, in theme `th`.

- `atlas: types.Texture = @fromBackingInt(2)`
- `small: Icon = .{ .texture = atlas, .uv = .{ 0, 0, 16.0 / 40.0, 16.0 / 24.0 }, .size = .{ 16, 16 } }`
- `large: Icon = .{ .texture = atlas, .uv = .{ 16.0 / 40.0, 0, 1, 1 }, .size = .{ 24, 24 } }`
- `Ui = base.ComptimeUi(State, spec, th)`
